## EVT-000158 - Profile 1.26: Skill Rank Ascension

```yaml
id: EVT-000158
canonical_record: REC-000079
schema_version: "0.1.5"
status: active
provenance:
  source: owner-ruling
  game_date: "2026-08-05 ~12:15 -05:00; profile adoption, no fictional time consumed"
  real_date: "2026-07-28"
type: Event
kind: system-profile-adoption
importance: structural
game_date: "2026-08-05 ~12:15 -05:00; profile adoption, no fictional time consumed"
participants:
  - ENT-000125
description: "Adopted frozen Gatefall World Rule Profile 1.26, which makes a skill's Rank a growth axis rather than a permanent property fixed at acquisition.

**The defect corrected.** Under 1.25 and earlier, a skill's Rank was set the moment it was learned and could rise only if loot happened to deliver the same technique at a higher Rank — a d8 boss-drop roll, a d10 book, or a d10 Premium rune — while Section 12.5 explicitly refused to sell a rune for a skill the Bearer already knew. Mastery raised power but never Rank. Because the Section 7.2 Rank baselines run 10/25/62/150/375/1000 while a full mastery track multiplies output by only ×1.3–×1.6, a fully mastered E-Rank technique was permanently worth less than a quarter of the same technique freshly learned at C-Rank. Alexander's own Rupture is the demonstration: E-Rank at Master, fourteen lifetime qualifying scenes, dealing 26 on a standard success, against 124 for a C-Rank Rupture learned that morning by a stranger. Learning a skill early was therefore strictly worse than declining to learn it and waiting for higher-Rank instruction — an incentive exactly opposed to the one a growth system should create.

**Two ascension routes (Section 7.5).** A known skill's Rank now rises by two routes and only these. **Breakthrough**, the earned road: a skill reaching Master mastery while below the ceiling is *offered* one Rank and settles at Adept, keeping three of five mastery levels. **Rune ascension**, the bought road: consuming instruction that teaches a known technique above its current Rank raises it to that item's teaching Rank and resets mastery to Novice. Both are offers presented with exact before-and-after values on every axis the skill uses, and neither consumes an item or settles a change unless accepted; a declined rune remains intact and owned. The earned road yields the stronger result at the same Rank — for Rupture, ×2.30 against ×2.00 — while the bought road is instant, needs no mastery prerequisite, and can cross several Ranks in one consumption.

**The ceiling.** No skill may exceed the Bearer's System Rank + 1, capped at S-Rank. At System Rank D the ceiling is C-Rank. The ceiling binds acquisition as well as ascension: instruction teaching above it does not consume and waits intact until the Bearer's System Rank rises, the same intact-item branch already used for ineligible NPC recipients. This is what removes the incentive to postpone learning — an unlearned skill and a learned one are bounded identically, and the learned one has been accumulating mastery and lifetime counters the whole time.

**The Rank Dominance Law (Section 7.2).** Utility skills receive a Rank scale sized so that one invariant holds for every skill in the profile regardless of category: a skill at Novice one Rank higher is stronger than the same skill at Master one Rank lower. Damage and healing satisfied this natively. Reduction skills now gain +25 percentage points per Rank above native against mastery's +20 across a whole track; passive multipliers gain +0.25 against mastery's +0.20; modifier-step skills saturate duration at whole-scene one Rank above native and spend every further Rank on scope — additional subjects, allies, patterns, or concurrent instances — because whole-scene is mastery's absolute ceiling and no duration-based step could exceed it. The single-skill reduction cap rises from 75% to 90%; at that cap further Rank steps convert to duration, so no ascension is ever worth nothing. Rank never changes a skill's Mana cost, which remains mastery's axis alone.

**Native Ranks declared.** Every skill now has a native Rank as the zero point of that scale. The eight Section 7.3 starting skills carry the native Ranks already listed there; Rupture and Bulwark, previously authored only in the Section 11.3 skill-book table with no floor, are declared native E-Rank; an earned technique's native Rank is the Rank at which it was ratified.

**Rune economy (Section 12.5).** The catalogue's blanket refusal to sell instruction for a known skill is lifted. The shop now stocks a rune for a skill at every Rank from its native Rank up to the ascension ceiling, and for a known skill only at Ranks above its current one. The C-through-S price anchors — 20,000, 90,000, 400,000 and 1,800,000 g — become live catalogue prices rather than Premium-only references. The Daily Premium tab's remaining advantages are its unbound-awakened binding and quantity-one scarcity; a Premium Rank roll landing above the ceiling now buys instruction the Bearer cannot yet consume and banks it.

**Counters.** Because qualifying_scenes_total never resets while mastery does, a skill's mastery level ceases to be derivable from its lifetime scene count once it has ascended. mastery_level becomes an authoritative world-declared tracked counter alongside the existing three, and rank_ascensions is added as a lifetime provenance counter that gates nothing. For every skill with no recorded ascension the stored mastery_level equals min(5, 1 + floor(qualifying_scenes_total ÷ 3)) — what it has always been — so adoption requires no recomputation.

**Folded correction — Keen Sense and Silent Step.** Section 7.3 authors both as lasting *for the scene*, but Section 7.4's mastery ladder lengthened a modifier-step skill by one exchange per level and reached whole-scene only at Master, which implied one exchange at Novice and made mastering these two appear to shorten them. Section 7.4 now states that a skill already scene-long at Novice has no duration to gain and spends mastery on **scope** instead — 1/2/3/4/5 beneficiaries from Novice through Master — that the authored Section 7.3 entry governs where the two disagree, and that **mastery never shortens an authored effect**. This is a rendering correction rather than a rule change, and the campaign record proves it: every recorded Keen Sense use already resolved scene-long. At `EVT-000114` a single 2-Mana activation at Novice ran across the corridor read, Owen's solo kill, a seven-common delegated sweep, and the elite read from the passage mouth, explicitly narrated as 'with Keen Sense still running'. Under the ladder's implied one-exchange Novice duration that sequence would have required repeated re-activations the Mana trace does not show. The shorter reading therefore never governed a resolved roll. Alexander's Keen Sense ledger line is corrected on three points that contradicted its own stated Adept level — the '★★★★☆' glyph, the 'for two exchanges at Adept' duration, and the 'toward Master' progress target — and gains the authored scope ladder, giving him himself plus two allies at Adept. No counter, mastery level, Mana cost, or resolved outcome changes. Silent Step is unlearned and gains nothing today.

**Adoption boundary.** This step is additive. Every one of Alexander's known skills stands at its native Rank at adoption, so the new utility Rank scales change no current value by construction. No skill changes Rank, mastery level, Mana cost, effect magnitude, or counter; no held item changes identity, Rank, binding, or quantity; no past consumption, purchase, or resolved roll is reopened. He holds no unused instructional item — only an unused Runic Key [E-Rank] — so the acquisition ceiling reprices nothing he owns. Ascension, the ceiling, and the opened rune catalogue apply prospectively only.

**Immediately consequential.** Rupture stands E-Rank at Master with its Rank below the C-Rank ceiling, and is therefore ascension-ready the moment play resumes: the System will present a breakthrough offer taking it to D-Rank Adept, ×2.30 against a 25 baseline, 57 on a standard success against its current 26. No other known skill is at Master. The offer stands open indefinitely and costs nothing to decline.

No fictional time, roll, gold, cash, owned item, skill value, pool, quest, damage result, or prior outcome changed. Checkpoint 0033 remains immutable at Profile 1.25 / Data Model 0.1.5 and restores through the applicable compatibility chain to Profile 1.26."
```

## EVT-000159 - Profile 1.27: Concealed-Canon Supply

```yaml
id: EVT-000159
canonical_record: REC-000079
schema_version: "0.1.5"
status: active
provenance:
  source: ruling
  game_date: "2026-08-05 ~12:15 -05:00"
  real_date: "2026-07-28"
type: Event
kind: ruling
importance: immediate
game_date: "2026-08-05 ~12:15 -05:00"
participants:
  - ENT-000125
description: "Gatefall World Rule Profile 1.26 → 1.27 adopted at the 2026-08-05 12:15 anchor. **Additive**: no fictional time, roll, gold, cash, owned item, skill value, pool, counter, quest, or prior outcome changed.

**The defect.** Across the campaign's full played span — fourteen in-fiction days, 2026-07-22 to 2026-08-05, and every day Section 8.4 has existed — the System has issued **zero Hidden quests and zero Urgent quests**. No audit was performed incorrectly. Section 8.4.3 makes a Hidden pointer eligible only when a concealed discovery *already exists in authored canon* with a recorded reveal condition, and the resident trigger audit is expressly forbidden to invent one. Gatefall had authored many secrets — Foss's shelved brand file, Deacon's off-band cores, the Cicero Scar's unexplained perimeter spikes, the Old Town Gate's receding clock — but not one of them carried a reveal condition, an objective, or any statement of what physical proximity to it meant, so not one could satisfy the section's fourth criterion. The filter was correct and the supply behind it was empty. Every audit that fired nothing was right to.

**The supply.** New world ledger `worlds/gatefall/230_KNOWLEDGE_SUBJECTS.md` (`REC-000100`) holds ten world-scope `concealed-discovery` records (`ENT-000151`–`ENT-000160`) with thirteen holder Knowledge States (`REL-000071`–`REL-000083`). These invent no new mysteries: each translates a secret Gatefall had already authored into the exact shape Section 8.4.3 consumes, and each is sited where an E- or D-Rank hunter working ordinary contracts can physically reach it. Six sit in Chicago, three in Prague, and one — the brand as one pattern — is the connective subject none of its four fragment-holders has ever set beside another's. Several bottom out in the World Bible's owner-only section; those carry `resolution_depth: surface`, state their reachable layer only, and leave the deep answer to an owner ruling at reveal.

**Campaign promotions.** Three threads this campaign's own fiction produced are promoted into eligible concealed canon in `110_WORLD_LEDGER.md` (`ENT-000161`–`ENT-000163`, `REL-000084`–`REL-000086`): the pawnbroker's withheld use for C-Rank-and-above cores, already flagged in canon as a live thread; what became of the Cicero scavenger who stopped taking calls; and the return half of Ferro's Reclamation's two-way favor trade with Coalition compliance — the half that determines whether Alexander's own name travels.

**The ritual (Section 8.4.5).** A Runtime may now author a concealed-discovery record during play, promoting an unresolved thread the fiction already produced. Three bounds: **time separation** — a record is written no earlier than the close of the scene that produced its thread, and its pointer may attach no earlier than a *later* scene, so a record authored and attached in one exchange remains manufacturing and stays prohibited under Section 14.3 Tier 3; **grounding** — the record anchors to something already established, never to an invented mystery; and **completeness before attachment** — the full field set including the reveal condition is written before any pointer attaches, so retroactive quest creation stays impossible. The section also fixes the field contract, forbids a record from storing a reward (Section 8.4.3 fixes that from System Rank at attachment), and holds world records immutable against play, with live pointer status remaining campaign state.

**What did not change.** Section 8.4.3's four criteria are untouched and all four still must hold. Real proximity or a genuinely resolved clue is still required; asking the System what `???` means still reveals nothing; a guess is still not a pointer; and Section 8.4.4 still forbids completion from conduct predating attachment. The change widens the supply of things to find, not the bar for finding them.

**Adoption boundary.** **No pointer attaches at adoption.** The fourteen days already played are not re-audited against the new ledger, and `system_state.non_daily_quests` is unchanged — capacity 0/2 with `active: []` and `pending_offers: []`. Every campaign-scope record's proximity condition is deliberately forward-looking: delivering a C-Rank-or-above core into the pawnbroker's hands, reaching the middleman or the Cicero recovery site, doing business inside Ferro's. Alexander holds a secondhand account of the Cicero remains and the scavenger's silence, which is neither first-hand proximity nor a resolved clue under the records as authored. Checkpoint 0033 remains immutable at Profile 1.25 / Data Model 0.1.5 and restores through the applicable compatibility chain to Profile 1.27."
```

## EVT-000160 - Profile 1.28: The Tracked Board

```yaml
id: EVT-000160
canonical_record: REC-000079
schema_version: "0.1.5"
status: active
provenance:
  source: ruling
  game_date: "2026-08-05 ~12:15 -05:00"
  real_date: "2026-07-28"
type: Event
kind: ruling
importance: immediate
game_date: "2026-08-05 ~12:15 -05:00"
participants:
  - ENT-000125
description: "Gatefall World Rule Profile 1.27 → 1.28 adopted at the 2026-08-05 12:15 anchor. **Additive**: no fictional time, roll, gold, cash, owned item, skill value, pool, counter, quest, or prior outcome changed.

**The defect.** Section 9.3 gives every Gate a break countdown and Section 9.8 authors the dungeon break as the disaster the entire regulatory apparatus exists to prevent — but nothing was ever counting. A Gate the Bearer was contracted to had a live clock because the contract put it in front of him; the Red Line Corridor posting of `EVT-000051` had one, and was cleared on its deadline at `EVT-000059`/`EVT-000060`. Every other detection in the metro entered a scene as a line on a board and ceased to exist when the scene ended. No Gate could break anywhere except on Alexander's own contract, which is also why Section 8.4.2 has had almost no input stream: its eligible crisis is a Gate break, a Gate creature, or a perceived sealed-instance hazard, and the only Gates with running clocks were the ones he was already being paid to clear — excluded by name as routine contracted hunting.

**Section 9.10 — the tracked board.** A detection that has entered the fiction through an ordinary channel the Bearer perceives — the BGM ranking board, a co-op or guild dispatch posting, an auction lot, or a named NPC telling him about one — now persists. It stores its place anchor, detection timestamp, assessed Rank and confidence, its Section 9.4 posting floor, a staffing status, and a break deadline **derived** from Section 9.3 rather than authored. Three to six tracked postings is the ordinary standing state for Chicago.

**This is persistence, not simulation.** Section 9.1's rates remain explicitly aggregate and explicitly not a calendar. The Runtime still never rolls the metro's ~35 weekly detections and still decides when a Gate enters the fiction; what changed is only that a Gate stops evaporating afterward. The board holds what the fiction has actually surfaced and nothing else, and the Runtime does not manufacture postings to fill it.

**Deadline resolution is deterministic** — no roll, no discretion. A posting `staffed` or `held` at its deadline clears off-screen, which is the ordinary case and the one that happens most. A posting `withdrawn` leaves the board. Only a posting still `posted` — under its floor when the clock runs out — breaks under Section 9.8. This is grounded in what Section 9.4 already says about the trade: cheap jobs sit, the under-strength waiver tier is where casualties concentrate, and `EVT-000051` records a real posting sitting undersubscribed at Red Line rates. `EVT-000052`'s auction war is authored to pull bids away from exactly these mid-tier lots. Institutions still act before the deadline — BGM escalates, raises the rate, or issues an emergency contract — and those interventions work by changing a posting's staffing status, which is how a break gets averted rather than decreed.

**A posting is tracked state, not a Persistent Object.** It carries a stable local board key and mints no identifier; most postings are cleared by other people and leave without ever having mattered individually. Promotion to an Event happens only when a posting becomes campaign-durable — Alexander bids, takes, or enters it, it breaks, or it produces a consequence the campaign will cite — exactly as `EVT-000051` carries the one posting that mattered.

**What this does not create.** A break is a world event, not a quest. If one fires where Alexander can perceive and reach it, Section 8.4.2's four criteria are then evaluated on their own terms and **unchanged**; the Runtime never adjusts a posting's staffing, deadline, or location to make an offer fit. If he is across the city it is news he hears, and the world is worse for it whether or not he was there. A posting two days from its deadline is information and pressure, never a summons.

**The board at adoption** holds four Chicago clearance postings, seeded in `110_WORLD_LEDGER.md` with their honest current staffing and nothing pre-decided: `GB-01`, a confirmed E-Rank in the Red Line Corridor detected 2026-08-01, breaking midnight 2026-08-08, four signed of five at waiver tier and five days unfilled; `GB-02`, an unconfirmed D-Rank on the Lakefront detected 2026-08-03, breaking midnight 2026-08-09, a mid-tier auction lot with thin bids; `GB-03`, a confirmed C-Rank on the Lakefront detected 2026-08-04, breaking midnight 2026-08-09, held by Horizon Guild dispatch and on track to clear; and `GB-04`, a confirmed E-Rank in the Red Line Corridor posted this morning, breaking midnight 2026-08-12 and filling normally. Every deadline is derived arithmetic, verified against `EVT-000051`'s own control case (detected 2026-07-18, unconfirmed D, six days, broke midnight 2026-07-24). These are clearance contracts and are consistent with `EVT-000155`, in which Kesha Morrison had no *mining or harvest* work at C-Rank or above — which is what Alexander had asked her for.

**No Gate breaks retroactively.** Every Gate named in a past scene is resolved exactly as that scene resolved it; no earlier posting is re-derived against Section 9.10; and no completed clear, filed split, or closed contract is reopened. Deadline resolution applies prospectively, from the first tracked deadline falling after adoption. Alexander's standing order that no crew Gate precedes the 2026-08-09 training session is a constraint of his own making covering Priya's crew, not a bar on freelance work he takes himself. Checkpoint 0033 remains immutable at Profile 1.25 / Data Model 0.1.5 and restores through the applicable compatibility chain to Profile 1.28."
```

## EVT-000161 - Profile 1.29: Urgent Eligibility Clarified

```yaml
id: EVT-000161
canonical_record: REC-000079
schema_version: "0.1.5"
status: active
provenance:
  source: ruling
  game_date: "2026-08-05 ~12:15 -05:00"
  real_date: "2026-07-28"
type: Event
kind: ruling
importance: immediate
game_date: "2026-08-05 ~12:15 -05:00"
participants:
  - ENT-000125
description: "Gatefall World Rule Profile 1.28 → 1.29 adopted at the 2026-08-05 12:15 anchor. **Additive**: no fictional time, roll, gold, cash, owned item, skill value, pool, counter, quest, or prior outcome changed.

**The defect.** Profiles 1.27 and 1.28 gave the Hidden and Urgent triggers a supply to fire from. This step corrects the last part of the same failure: two clauses of Section 8.4.2 that were being read more broadly than they were written, which is why Urgent stayed at zero even in scenes that had real crises in them.

**Criterion 1 never said whether the Bearer counts.** It requires 'at least one non-hostile person' at immediate threat of death. Read one way the Bearer qualifies, and an offer fires in every lethal fight he has — plainly not the intent, since criterion 3's 'intervene' means acting on someone else's behalf. Read the other way he does not, which is what a careful Runtime already assumed but the section never confirmed. It now states it: the imperilled person is someone **other than the Bearer**, and danger to his own life is combat under Sections 6 and 20.4, never a quest. The System does not offer him a reward for surviving.

**The contract exclusion was written to stop every contract becoming a quest, and read as covering everything that happens during one.** It is now scoped to what it meant: the contracted objective itself, and danger to any person who entered under the **same contract** as the Bearer, strike and support element alike. A crew facing the Gate's population is doing the insured job it signed for, and the System does not pay a bonus for a hunter doing his own job. The exclusion does **not** reach a person who is not party to that contract — a civilian inside a cordon, a bystander at a break site, an unrelated crew, or a survey team the contract does not cover. Where a qualifying threat puts one of them at immediate death, that is not routine by any reading, and criteria 1–4 are evaluated on their own terms.

**Sealed-instance reachability is now stated rather than inferred.** The earlier working assumption that sealed instances are bystander-free is wrong on the profile's own text: Section 17 permits the Bearer to bring a party into an instant dungeon, and Section 9.6's red gate seals whoever entered it. Criterion 1 can therefore hold inside a sealed instance where he brought others in. Where he entered **alone** it never can, and a solo instant dungeon is permanently Urgent-ineligible — a deliberate consequence, not an oversight. The more of his dangerous time Alexander spends in private instances of his own opening, the less of his life is Urgent-eligible. Solitude buys safety from the world's claims on him and costs him the world's opportunities. This campaign supplies both cases: Alexander has opened **eleven** instant dungeons (`EVT-000097`, `EVT-000099`, `EVT-000101`, `EVT-000109`, `EVT-000113`, `EVT-000142`, `EVT-000144`, and `EVT-000149`–`EVT-000152`), ten of them alone and therefore Urgent-ineligible under this clarification — and one that is the counter-example proving the branch is live rather than dead: at `EVT-000144` the key opened the Crypt **around both him and Owen**, who was inside, geared, and told to watch rather than fight, for the whole clear.

**What did not change.** The crisis bar itself. All four criteria still must hold: a present and nearby crisis, a Gate creature or active break or perceived sealed-instance hazard, a physically actionable route to intervene before it resolves, and an objective, success condition, failure condition and causal deadline all statable from what he already perceives. The System still does not reveal an unconfirmed Gate Rank, a hidden attacker, or an NPC's intent to make an offer fit. A danger he deliberately created, a report he cannot reach in time, property loss without danger to life, and an already-resolved crisis still do not qualify. Reward, capacity, acceptance and lifecycle are untouched.

**No retroactive offers.** No past scene is re-audited against the narrowed exclusion, no completed contract, clear or resolved crisis is reopened, and no offer is reconstructed for anyone who was in peril in an earlier session. Eligibility applies prospectively from the first audit after adoption. `non_daily_quests` stays 0/2 with empty `active` and `pending_offers`. Checkpoint 0033 remains immutable at Profile 1.25 / Data Model 0.1.5 and restores through the applicable compatibility chain to Profile 1.29."
```

**Before → after.** Rupture [E-Rank] ★★★★★ Master — ×2.60 of the E-Rank baseline (10), 26 damage on a standard success, Mana 8 — becomes Rupture [D-Rank] ★★★☆☆ Adept — ×2.30 of the D-Rank baseline (25), 57 damage on a standard success, Mana 10. `mastery_progress` resets to 0/3 toward Expert. `successful_uses` (30) and `qualifying_scenes_total` (14) are lifetime totals and carry forward unchanged. `rank_ascensions` 0 → 1.

This is a player-accepted System offer settled under Turn-State Settlement, not a dangerous-scene or promotion-batch audit — it required no qualifying scene of its own, only the mastery advance already recorded at `EVT-000150` and the ceiling already open since System Rank crossed to D at that same event."

**What the read produced.** Dolly-wheel wear on the side-door ramp inconsistent with foot or hand-truck traffic and scuffed fresh, within the hour; a sharp mineral smell at the doorframe matching an unstabilized mana crystal before appraisal clearance; a torn corner of impact-cushioning packing material bearing a partial stencil mark, unrecognized. **Concealed-discovery authored, not attached.** Per Section 8.4.5's promotion ritual, `ENT-000165` ('what the intake storefront actually moves') is authored this event from a thread the prior scene (`EVT-000163`) already produced — grounded, complete, time-separated. No Hidden pointer attaches in this same exchange; attachment requires a later scene's audit to find the proximity or clue condition met, which it is not yet (Alexander has read the exterior only, not entered, transacted, or traced the stencil).

**Settlement:** Keen Sense `successful_uses` 6→7, `qualifying_scenes_total` 6→7, `mastery_progress` 0→1/3 toward Expert. 5 minutes elapsed (12:35→12:40); Mana 24→25 (recovery) → 23 (Keen Sense's 2-Mana cost)."

One building stood out: a blank, unsignposted warehouse behind a conspicuously new chain-link fence with fresh razor wire and a commercial biometric-capable keypad gate. Activated Keen Sense a second time (Mana 2) to read it. 🎲 d100 65, net +1 step, effective 85 — strong success.

Reads as real capital protecting *conditions* inside, not just contents — a different character from the storefront's quiet cargo-moving. No one observed entering or leaving.

**Concealed-discovery authored, not attached.** `ENT-000166` (the warehouse) and `ENT-000167` ('what the fenced warehouse protects') authored this event per Section 8.4.5 — grounded in this scene, time-separated from any future attachment. No Hidden pointer attaches this exchange; proximity/clue conditions require actual entry or a confirmed contact, not an exterior read.

**No trigger fired** beyond the above. No Urgent condition. Non-daily slots remain 0/2.

## EVT-000166 - Coffee, Kesha, and the Lakefront Hold

Handed her a coffee; she read through his 'nothing much' immediately and asked what he actually wanted. He admitted, honestly, that he was bored and job-hunting. She offered the sitting Red Line Corridor waiver-tier posting (confirmed E-Rank, four of five hunters signed — Dax Reilly, a mender-adjacent duo partner going by Priest, and Hollis Vance). Alexander declined a stranger crew, then clarified he meant a **Cicero-style two-handed sponsored booking** with Owen again, not sending Owen in alone or untrained. Kesha checked and found a **fresh confirmed E-Rank Gate at the Lakefront Gate Corridor**, detected that morning, not yet publicly posted. She agreed to hold it — Owen as sponsor of record, same terms as Cicero, no five-hunter floor — until **22:00 tonight**; unconfirmed by then, it goes up on the public board tomorrow morning. `OBJ-17` opened.

Also reconfirmed: the ≈$40,000 Denise Ferro rail-corridor payout is still processing, ordinary 1–2 business days, no new information. Conversation closed warmly; Kesha returned to the counter to handle the next hunter in line.

**No trigger fired.** No Urgent or Hidden condition met. Non-daily System quest slots remain 0/2 (this is an ordinary Coalition-booked contract, not a System quest).

**Settlement:** 50 minutes elapsed (13:15→14:05); Mana 25→31/72 (recovery only, no skill used)."
```

## EVT-000167 - Denise Ferro and Milo's Cones

description: "Checked his phone for Dale Pruitt's promised answer on the B-Rank mining lead — nothing yet, and correctly recalled the actual deadline is end of day tomorrow (2026-08-06), not today.

Spotted Denise Ferro finishing a crew sign-off at the appraisal pavilion and waited rather than interrupt. She was surprised, pleasantly, that he'd waited her out; her crew is between jobs (still chasing paperwork on the rail-corridor job), nothing to offer today, but named him near the top of her list for future work needing 'a hitter who doesn't need his hand held.' Parted warmly.

Walked to the training grounds. Tanya Voss was visible running a drill with newer hunters but was not approached or noticed — a sprinter running reaction footwork between cones caught his attention first. Introduced himself to the sprinter, **Milo** (`ENT-000168`, new) — sensor-adjacent, minor perception ability, no notable combat skill, trains reaction footwork as compensation. Ran Milo's drill twice at his invitation, cold, no prior exposure to the method.

**First pass:** 🎲 d100 88, net +1 (raw athleticism/reflexes), effective 100 (clamped) — legendary success. Clean, unbroken run, no hesitation.
**Second pass** (Milo raised the difficulty — faster calls, feints added): 🎲 d100 73, net +1, effective 93 — strong success. Caught one feint half a beat late, weight already committed wrong, but recovered mid-stride without losing the run.

Milo was visibly startled both times, joked that it looked like Alexander was reading his mind rather than his feet; accepted Alexander's deflection ('I like running') without pushing. Invited him back anytime. Not mechanically tracked — no authored skill covers this, recorded as qualitative demonstrated capability only (`100_CHARACTER_SHEET.md`).

**No trigger fired.** No Urgent or Hidden condition. Non-daily slots remain 0/2.

**Settlement:** 35 minutes elapsed (14:05→14:40); Mana 31→35/72 (recovery only, no skill used). Scene closes with Alexander back in the open Coalition lot, empty coffee cup in hand, no destination decided."
```

participants:
  - ENT-000125
description: "Gatefall World Rule Profile 1.29 → 1.30 adopted at the 2026-08-05 14:40 anchor. **Additive**: no fictional time, roll, gold, cash, owned item, skill value, pool, counter, quest, or prior outcome changed.

**The defect.** Profiles 1.27, 1.28 and 1.29 fixed the dead Hidden and Urgent triggers. This step fixes the reason the fault survived a whole campaign undetected: **nothing counted the zeros**. Every proactive audit was performed correctly and recorded honestly, each one reporting that no condition arose — and no artifact anywhere aggregated those reports into the observation that none ever had. The gap surfaced because the player noticed it, not because the repository said so.

**Section 8.4.6 — trigger telemetry.** Each checkpoint now writes a `trigger_telemetry` block into mutable live state: lifetime Hidden pointer attachments and Urgent offers, the campaign timestamp of the most recent of each, the in-fiction days since (or since campaign start where neither has ever happened), and the two **supply** counts — concealed discoveries available under Section 8.4.5, and live postings on the Section 9.10 board.

**The supply counts are the point.** A dry count alone is uninterpretable, and reading it wrongly is how the original defect persisted. Supply zero against a rising dry count is the design fault: no amount of correct auditing can produce a quest from an empty supply, and the response is to author concealed canon or let the board surface postings, at the owner's pace, between sessions. Supply healthy against a rising dry count is **not a fault at all** — it means the world has things to find and the Bearer has not yet met a proximity, clue, or crisis condition, and the correct response is none. A checkpoint recording a rising dry count against healthy supply is reporting a working system.

**Diagnostic, never a trigger.** Nothing in the section creates, permits, or obliges a quest. No value it records is a precondition; no threshold releases the Runtime from Section 8.4.2 or 8.4.3; and a long dry count is never grounds to attach a pointer, issue an offer, invent a crisis, or lower a bar. A rule firing a quest at a dry-spell threshold would be a Tier-3 no-declared-precondition firing wearing a counter as a disguise (Section 14.3), and is prohibited exactly as such. The Runtime writes these counters and does not act on them; the owner reads them, between sessions, about the design.

**Not System state.** The System's assertive domain is closed (Sections 14.1, 14.4) and holds nothing about the health of its own trigger supply. The block lives in campaign state, never in `system_state`, and never renders in any `/system` panel. Alexander cannot see it and cannot ask.

**First reading, at this anchor — the healthy zero, not the broken one.** `hidden_pointers_attached: 0`, `urgent_offers_issued: 0`, both dry counts at **14** in-fiction days from the 2026-07-22 campaign start. But `concealed_records_available: 15` — ten world-scope in `230_KNOWLEDGE_SUBJECTS.md` and five campaign-scope in `110_WORLD_LEDGER.md`, none attached or completed — and `tracked_postings: 4`. The same two zeros that signalled a broken engine before Profile 1.27 now signal a working one. This afternoon's own span is the demonstration: two concealed records were authored from genuinely resolved Keen Sense reads (`ENT-000165`, the storefront's operator; `ENT-000167`, the fenced warehouse's purpose), and neither attached a pointer, because both are exterior reads with no entry, transaction, or traced contact. That is Section 8.4.3's bar holding, and it needs no fix.

**Immutable checkpoints are not retrofitted.** Captures predating this section stay byte-unchanged and carry no telemetry block; the counters begin at the first checkpoint written after adoption and count from campaign start. Adoption attaches no pointer, issues no offer, and re-audits no past scene. Checkpoint 0034 remains immutable at Profile 1.25 / Data Model 0.1.5 and restores through the applicable compatibility chain to Profile 1.30."
```

Ordered delivery burgers and beer rather than go anywhere. Over dinner, half-joking, Owen said 'Marry me'; called on it, he confirmed he meant the thing itself, not the timing. Alexander's unhedged 'Yes. Eventually. Du'h' visibly caught him off guard — first time either has raised marriage in any register, roughly two weeks into the relationship. 'I love you' repeated once more over the same meal. Kesha Morrison texted directly and declined the held Lakefront booking (`OBJ-17` closed) rather than ask Owen to take a job after the day he'd had; she released it without friction, reposting publicly the morning of 2026-08-06. Owen fell asleep on the couch during a background movie, unaware of the text; Alexander cleaned up quietly, then woke him gently and walked him to bed. Mana climbed to its maximum (72/72) partway through the evening via ordinary active recovery; remainder reset to zero per Section 5.2. No Stat, XP, gold, or item change."

## EVT-000175 - Promotion Barrier: Skill-Formation Batch Settlement

```yaml
id: EVT-000175
canonical_record: REC-000079
schema_version: "0.1.5"
status: active
provenance:
  source: ruling
  game_date: "2026-08-06 ~11:15 -05:00"

importance: minor

participants:

progression_audits:
  - subject: ENT-000125
    domain: gatefall.skill_formation
    result: evidence-recorded
    candidate: reactive_pattern_anticipation
    scene: milo-footwork-drill-cold-double-pass
    disposition: qualifying
description: "The first promotion-barrier batch settlement this campaign has written, discharging Profile Section 7.1's deferred half for the thirteen play Events since the `EVT-000153` baseline (`EVT-000155`-`EVT-000157`, `EVT-000163`-`EVT-000167`, `EVT-000169`-`EVT-000173`). Section 7.1 requires each barrier to create one canon-bearing `progression-batch-settlement` Event carrying the required audits, including an explicit `none` when nothing qualifies. No barrier had ever created one — neither settlement kind appears as an Event `kind` anywhere in this or any other campaign — so sealed work and practice notes had nowhere to go and a qualifying practice scene was indistinguishable from a non-qualifying one. That is how `EVT-000167` was missed until an owner re-read it by hand (`EVT-000174`).

**Classification of the batch.** One scene qualifies: `EVT-000167`'s Milo reaction-footwork drill, already opened as `reactive_pattern_anticipation` at `EVT-000174` and recorded here as the batch's `evidence-recorded` result rather than counted twice. Its candidate state, evidence pointer, and 1-of-3 progress are unchanged by this Event.

Every other scene in the span classifies `none`, and for reasons Section 7.1 states rather than by default. `EVT-000155`-`EVT-000157`, `EVT-000163`, `EVT-000166`, `EVT-000169`, `EVT-000170` and `EVT-000173` are conversation, errand, and relationship scenes demonstrating no repeatable method under tested execution. `EVT-000164` and `EVT-000165` are Keen Sense reads — a known ratified skill, so they settle as Section 7.4 use counters, which they did, and open no formation candidate. `EVT-000171` is rest and the daily quest issuing. `EVT-000172` is the doubled solo regimen: the nearest call in the batch, and still `none`, because doubling an automatic daily checklist adds volume to a task whose outcome is already assured rather than supplying the bounded objective, uncertain execution, or informative resistance Section 7.1 requires to distinguish improvement from repetition.

No fictional time, roll, counter, skill value, item, pool, or resolved outcome changes. This Event records a classification that was owed and never written; it authors no new fiction.

**Recovery derivation for the span, restored 2026-07-30 from checkpoint 0042's still-immutable copy after a later ledger compression dropped it from live state without a surviving live copy.** 20h35m elapsed from the 2026-08-05 14:40 anchor, remainder carried in at 648,000 from `EVT-000167`'s settlement (`900_CHECKPOINT_0034`). `EVT-000169` (14:40→15:15, 35 min, active): 648,000 + 2,100 × 72 × 20 = 3,672,000 units → +5 Mana, remainder 72,000 (Mana 35 → 40), then a repeat Keen Sense activation (🎲 d100 20, failure, no new information), 2-Mana cost (40 → 38). `EVT-000170` (15:15→21:30, 6h15m, active — waiting, the drive, dinner, cleaning, bed): 72,000 + 22,500 × 72 × 20 = 32,472,000 units → pool reaches maximum before the span ends (Mana 38 → 72, capped), remainder reset to 0 per Section 5.2. `EVT-000171` (21:30→06:00, 8h30m resting overnight): pool already full, remainder stays 0; Health unaffected, already full. `EVT-000172` (06:00→~10:30, the thirteenth daily quest doubled solo): no Mana cost (physical regimen only), pool remains full. `EVT-000173` (~10:30→11:15, the lake swim): no Mana cost, pool remains full. Health was at maximum throughout the entire span, so its remainder stays 0 (Profile Section 6.1.1). Mode remains `active`."
```

## EVT-000176 - The Lake: Two Private Operations Collide

```yaml
id: EVT-000176
canonical_record: REC-000079
schema_version: "0.1.5"
status: active
provenance:
  source: transcript
  game_date: "2026-08-06 ~11:15-11:45 -05:00"
  real_date: "2026-07-28"
type: Event
kind: relationship
importance: major
game_date: "2026-08-06 ~11:15-11:45 -05:00"
participants:
  - ENT-000125
  - ENT-000139
counter_deltas: []
progression_audits:
  - subject: ENT-000125
    domain: gatefall.skill_formation
    result: none
description: "Continuing directly from `EVT-000173`'s anchor, treading water off the Loyola Park lakefront with Owen Callahan (`ENT-000139`).

**The clock.** Alexander asserted it was 'not even ten'; Owen corrected him to a quarter past eleven, reading the time off the size of the beach crowd — three summers of lifeguarding this exact stretch. Owen then volunteered, unprompted, that he had moved his 10:00 mender round to an afternoon block at 06:40 that morning after finding the note, and would be doing the Edgewater run and the intake backlog in one shift as a result.

**The accounting, both directions.** Owen's position: the note read *you know where I am*, which is a coordinate rather than an invitation, so he read it as Alexander wanting the road to himself and deliberately stayed away — stating plainly, as a limit volunteered without being asked, that he does not know how to be invited by a man who does not invite people, and would rather guess wrong toward leaving Alexander alone than toward crowding him. **A character first (Decision 076): the first time Owen has named that fear aloud.** Alexander's position, given plainly: the note *was* the invitation, he waited half an hour with coffee made, and left only when Owen did not stir. Owen then reconstructed his own morning and found the mug — full, cold, on his own counter — which he had poured out at half past ten believing Alexander had made it for himself and forgotten it. He conceded the larger point without hedging: he had spent nine hours in a room with Alexander the previous evening and never once asked how his day had been. He asked for one rule going forward — **wake him even when he looks like death, and let him be the one who says he cannot** — which Alexander accepted, immediately qualifying it with *once I commit you cannot say no.*

**The play-fight.** Alexander jumped Owen and dunked him, calling on him to fight his way out. Owen's escape resolved d100 96 — a natural critical success, read from the natural roll and unmodifiable (Rules Section 4.6): he went limp and submerged, breaking the grip entirely because deep water offers no leverage against a body that refuses to be a lever, surfaced behind Alexander and set a textbook cross-chest rescue hold. Play only; no damage, no Health change, no injury. **Overpower is not counted as a material application** — the target was a non-hostile partner in play, and the grapple was broken rather than resolved, so Section 7.4's 'resolved action in which its effect materially applies' is not met.

Both men named the shared defect explicitly: each is running a private one-man operation about not burdening the other. No canon-bearing state changed. Health 144/144, Mana 72/72 throughout."
```

## EVT-000177 - Nikitas: The Guard Table, and the Rocks

```yaml
id: EVT-000177
canonical_record: REC-000079
schema_version: "0.1.5"
status: active
provenance:
  source: transcript
  game_date: "2026-08-06 ~11:50-13:15 -05:00"
  real_date: "2026-07-28"
type: Event
kind: relationship
importance: major
game_date: "2026-08-06 ~11:50-13:15 -05:00"
participants:
  - ENT-000125
  - ENT-000139
  - ENT-000169
  - ENT-000170
counter_deltas: []
progression_audits:
  - subject: ENT-000125
    domain: gatefall.skill_formation
    result: none
description: "Walked four blocks north up Sheridan to **Nikitas** (`ENT-000170`, new), a Greek diner mid-block between a currency exchange and a shuttered storefront, sign reading NIK TAS with the eta burnt out. Alexander went shirtless the whole way after Owen told him the dress code was sand.

**Voula (`ENT-000169`, new).** The proprietor recognized Owen on the bell and named him without hesitation, correcting his 'nine years' to twelve — nine is when the last of the guard staff stopped coming, which is not the same thing. She fed the lifeguard staff across those summers on an off-menu plate for four dollars and stopped charging some of them at all while lying about it. She cleared the corner booth by the kitchen — **the guard table**, unseated for years and used to store newspapers and ketchup — in front of the whole room, ordered for both men without consulting them, and refused Alexander's money at the end. Asked to introduce himself, Alexander gave only his name; she registered the closed door and did not push.

**The laminated notice.** A sun-bleached memorial sheet taped inside the door at eye level, which Owen stopped short of and did arithmetic about. Voula volunteered the answer herself rather than let him be tactful for twenty minutes: her sister's boy, **Andreas**, two years ago, and specifically *not the lake*. The notice stays up.

**Owen's account of the rocks — the largest disclosure he has made in this campaign.** August of his third guarding summer, two teenagers off the rocks north of the buoy line in turning weather. He gave the outcome first: both pulled out, the girl worked on for eleven minutes on the sand, both alive, a card sent to the fieldhouse afterward. The part he had told nobody, including two paid therapists: on the rocks, for something between one and three seconds, **he did not go in.** In that gap he went for the boy — nearer, conscious, easier — and the other guard, Ruiz, went past him for the girl out where the bar pulls south. He has never been able to determine whether that was triage or fear wearing triage's clothes, and the reason it is unresolvable is that **it worked**: she lived, nobody reviewed it, nobody noticed there had been a pause, and his supervisor bought them both a beer.

He never worked a beach again, went into medicine and then into mending — described in his own words as a job where the going-in has already been done by someone braver, where you arrive after, and where the sums are slow enough to make with your hands instead of in a second and a half on wet rock. He then connected it to the Ashfield Gate himself, unled: he was too slow to reach Alexander at `EVT-000079` and apologized at the time, and has carried both since. **His stated fear is not that he will fail to reach Alexander — it is that he will pause again, it will work out again, and he will never know.** Alexander listened without pressing and did not offer absolution.

Skill-formation audit: `none`. Conversation and a meal; no repeatable method under tested execution."
```

## EVT-000178 - The Training Contract, and Owen's Second Ability

```yaml
id: EVT-000178
canonical_record: REC-000079
schema_version: "0.1.5"
status: active
provenance:
  source: transcript
  game_date: "2026-08-06 ~13:15-13:52 -05:00"
  real_date: "2026-07-28"
type: Event
kind: promotion
importance: major
game_date: "2026-08-06 ~13:15-13:52 -05:00"
participants:
  - ENT-000125
  - ENT-000139
counter_deltas: []
progression_audits:
  - subject: ENT-000125
    domain: gatefall.skill_formation
    result: none
description: "Outside Nikitas and then in Owen's car and flat in Rogers Park. Owen asked, unprompted, to be *trained* — explicitly not absolved — naming the thing he wanted as 'the pause', and specifying version two of the offer: put in front of something that can actually hurt him, not shown another demonstration from behind Alexander's shoulder.

**Disclosure.** Alexander volunteered, without being asked and when it could have stayed buried permanently, that the Lakefront two-handed booking closed as declined at `EVT-000170` had been set up the previous afternoon precisely as this training scenario, and was cancelled by text when Owen came through the door after a rough shift. Owen's response was to name it as the same paternalism he had objected to in the lake **and** to concede that he would have said yes and been useless — accepting both as true, and noting that the disclosure itself was the version he had asked for, twelve hours late. Owen, checking his own record, further established that Alexander had not been asked about his day at all the previous evening.

**The intervention threshold.** Alexander set the standard cold: he engages only when Owen would die if he did not; Owen would be allowed to take damage and to bleed. Owen accepted the standard and then supplied the *observable proxies* for it, on the stated grounds that 'would die' is a conclusion arriving about four seconds after the moment intervention was needed — which is what a pause is made of. **The four tells, in Owen's own words:** (1) down and not up inside three seconds; (2) three or more in contact at once and not broken on the next beat; (3) a hit after which his hands come open — both of them, which is blood pressure and means roughly eight seconds to useless; (4) still standing and not answering, which he named as the real one because he has never in his life stopped talking. Agreed as a rule to be read off him and executed without judgment in the moment.

**Owen learns Stone Skin — the gift.** Alexander materialized the banked rune teaching Stone Skin (boss drop from the 2026-08-05 Shattered City instant dungeon, `EVT-000152`, instruction_binding unbound-awakened) and gave it to Owen. Consumption settlement under Profile Section 7.1: eligible by binding, recipient is awakened and does not know the technique, so consumption teaches it; effective learned Rank is min(teaching_rank, recipient fixed Rank), and the rune teaches at Stone Skin's native **D-Rank** floor against Owen's C-Rank, so **Owen holds Stone Skin [D-Rank] at Novice — 30% physical damage reduction while sustained.** It draws on his own capacity, which is the same well his mending draws from; Owen identified that trade himself before consuming, naming it as the rocks made into a decision he takes six times a fight instead of once in a lifetime, and consumed it anyway. There was no window, no notification and nothing to see — he is not a Bearer and perceives no System (Profile Section 14.4); the capability was simply present afterward, the way his mending is.

**This is the first recorded instance in Gatefall of an awakened acquiring a second ability.** Owen's stated understanding, from BGM intake at twenty-two, is that an awakened's gift is fixed and singular for life, and that nobody has ever received another. Character Knowledge, unverified against any institution.

**New objective OBJ-18** — Alexander stated flatly, as a practical commitment rather than a sentiment, that he will find a way to enlarge Owen's capacity. Owen's recorded response was that he believes him, and that he does not yet know what it will cost Alexander to make it true.

Alexander equipped his full nine-slot loadout instantly on the landing (Section 12.9, free action outside an exchange) and gave Owen five minutes to physically dress in the Horizon gear bought at `EVT-000117`. No Mana, Health, XP, gold or Stat change in this Event; the Stone Skin rune leaves Alexander's holdings."
```

## EVT-000179 - The Overgrown Temple: Bought, Opened, Read

```yaml
id: EVT-000179
canonical_record: REC-000079
schema_version: "0.1.5"
status: active
provenance:
  source: transcript
  game_date: "2026-08-06 ~13:52-14:00 -05:00"
  real_date: "2026-07-28"
type: Event
kind: exploration
importance: major
game_date: "2026-08-06 ~13:52-14:00 -05:00"
participants:
  - ENT-000125
  - ENT-000139
counter_deltas:
  - { subject: ENT-000125, counter: skills.keen_sense.successful_uses, delta: 1 }
  - { subject: ENT-000125, counter: skills.exploit_pattern.successful_uses, delta: 1 }
description: "Alexander bought an **Instant-Dungeon Key [E-Rank]** from the standard System catalogue for **500 g** (Profile Section 12.5 key table), taking gold **507 to 7**, and opened the instance from the middle of Owen's kitchen — the same room the note was written in that morning. E-Rank was chosen deliberately over his own D-Rank tier as the safe training bracket; a D-Rank key at 2,250 g was unaffordable in any case.

**Instance generation (Profile Section 17).** Archetype d8 **4 → Overgrown Temple** (a lootable shrine granting a bonus loot roll on clear, guarded by an extra elite). Population at the key's E-Rank on the Section 9.7 formula: 3d6 = **11 common** (40 Health each), 1d2 = **1 elite**, plus the archetype's extra elite = **2 elites** (80 Health each), and **1 boss** (160 Health). Mineable deposit 3d6 = **11 E-Rank crystals**, unextracted. The instance runs two hours from opening — closing **15:55** — or on the boss kill, whichever comes first.

**The interior.** System architecture rather than a slice of the beasts' world (Section 10, instant-dungeon exception): cut stone in machine-regular courses, a colonnade on a grid running back roughly eighty metres, uniform sourceless light casting no shadow, floor tessellated in a pattern repeating without variation. Pale jointed translucent growth branches through the columns and vaulting at even intervals. The only non-repeating element in the visible interior is the raised shrine block at the far end, with the growth wound thickest around it.

**Keen Sense, on the threshold.** 2 Mana (Mana 72 to 70), d100 **11**, net +1 step (Keen Sense) +1 (Perception 38 against an E-Rank interior), effective **51 — partial success.** Read cleanly: eleven commons distributed through the grid, moving in the lattice rather than on the floor; one heavier mass pacing a transverse aisle at forty metres. The shrine read as *occupied* by a single dense mass and then greyed out — **the far end of the room was not bought by two Mana, and Alexander said so aloud to Owen rather than pretend otherwise.** At Adept the read extends to himself plus two allies, so Owen received the whole layout as if he had made it personally — the first time in his life he has perceived a Gate interior the way a sensor does. One successful use; the qualifying scene and mastery progress settle at dangerous-scene close, which has not occurred.

**Deliberate tactical read (Exploit Pattern).** Alexander explicitly declined to trust the Keen Sense result at face value and did his own analysis of the structure. d100 **61**, net +2 steps, effective clamp(101) = **100 — legendary success.** Two Patterns established, within Practiced's two-concurrent capacity:

1. **The lattice is their road.** Nothing travels the floor. The ropes branch at fixed intervals, so transit lines are countable: exactly four drop points into any bay between columns, all overhead, each requiring a committed drop. A man standing mid-bay cannot be flanked — only dropped on, from four marked places, sequentially. The floor is dead ground.
2. **The shrine holds more than one thing** — derived from geometry rather than the read. The colonnade repeats without variation, and the only place lattice density rises is around the shrine. Thicker lattice means more traffic means more feet. Alexander's own arithmetic recovered what the failed portion of the magical read had not.

One activation, one use (Section 7.4: one activation counts once regardless of how many targets or findings it affects)."
```

## EVT-000180 - Owen Clears the Bays

```yaml
id: EVT-000180
canonical_record: REC-000079
schema_version: "0.1.5"
status: active
provenance:
  source: transcript
  game_date: "2026-08-06 ~14:00-14:52 -05:00"
  real_date: "2026-07-28"
type: Event
kind: combat
importance: major
game_date: "2026-08-06 ~14:00-14:52 -05:00"
participants:
  - ENT-000125
  - ENT-000139
counter_deltas:
  - { subject: ENT-000125, counter: skills.field_command.successful_uses, delta: 3 }
description: "The training scenario as specified: **Owen fought the entire span alone.** Alexander took no offensive action, held the four agreed tells, and supported by relaying Patterns.

**Field Command's first material use in the campaign.** Alexander relayed Pattern 1 — the bay, the four drop points, the dead floor — to Owen by call, and Owen received it as personally understood (+1 modifier step on actions genuinely exploiting it). Novice supports one recipient. Coaching delivered alongside it and outside the mechanic: feet set, knife low and in, eyes on the ropes rather than the target, and the correction to the four-inch backward flinch diagnosed at `EVT-000116` — **not 'do not flinch' but 'flinch forward'**, moving into the drop, because a falling animal cannot correct past its own commitment. Owen was instructed to narrate the room aloud continuously, which doubles as tell (4).

**First contact.** Four commons committed drops on the threshold bay. Owen's answer d100 **48**, net +1 (relayed Pattern), effective **68 — success**: he moved forward into the first drop and killed it outright with the Boss-Imprinted Quickknife (`EVT-000119`). The remaining three, already in motion, resolved the same exchange — d100 **24**, net −1, effective **4 — critical failure**: all three had aimed at where a flinching man would have been, one landing on its own dead pack-mate, one going over on a column base with a limb bent under it, one snarled in the lattice six feet up.

**The sweep.** d100 **93**, net +1, effective **100 — legendary success.** Ten further commons across roughly thirty minutes, bay by bay, none of them touching him. **By the sixth bay Owen was calling the commits a half-beat ahead of Alexander** — narrating the whole room to himself in a flat clipped register that is recognisably Alexander's instructor voice. He stopped sounding frightened somewhere around the eighth.

**The elite.** It did not drop; it came along the floor at speed and never committed, which is precisely the limit of the Pattern. d100 **60**, net 0, effective **60 — partial success**: killed with the Quickknife under the jaw after roughly four minutes of grinding work in a bay too small for it, Owen pushed off the column twice and declining the aisle each time because he had been told not to take it. **He was not physically hurt.** The elite's own answer resolved d100 **54**, net −1, effective **34 — failure**; it never landed a serious blow. The partial success's cost fell on his capacity instead: **roughly two-thirds of Owen's mending well spent**, most of it on sustaining Stone Skin — its first-ever use, and the first time an impact that should have landed did not do what it should. His own estimate afterward: one ordinary patient left in him, at most.

**XP to Alexander under Section 3.8.** Kill XP goes to the Bearer for any kill his live-die actions materially contributed to, explicitly including 'a control or setup action the resolved exchange turned on'. The legendary Pattern read and its relay were live-die actions the exchanges turned on, so the eleven commons (10 each) and the elite (10 x 2) credit: **+120 XP, 620 to 740/1000.** No underdog multiplier — E-Rank creatures at or below his D-Rank System Rank (Section 3.3). No level threshold crossed. Alexander's own participation is a live question going forward: from roughly the sixth bay Owen was exploiting the Pattern without needing the call.

Field Command counted **3 material applications** — one per resolved action in which its effect materially applied (first contact, the compressed sweep, the elite). Qualifying scene and mastery progress settle at dangerous-scene close, which has not occurred.

**The Mana potion — a campaign discovery.** With Owen's well spent and two masses rising at the shrine, Alexander opened the shop, sold **all 112 banked E-Rank crystals** at 10 g each (**+1,120 g**, gold 7 to 1,127), bought one **Standard Mana Potion** for **75 g** (Profile 1.25 price; restores a flat 40 Mana), gold **1,127 to 1,052**, and handed it to Owen with a one-word instruction. Owen drank it without asking a single question.

**It restored him fully.** Forty is over half a D-Rank Bearer's entire pool and comfortably exceeds a C-Rank mender's reserve. Owen's stated reaction: capacity does not come back like that — it returns overnight or across a day, and he named it as the one hard constraint on the entire profession. **Established this Event: a System Mana potion restores an awakened non-Bearer's expended capacity.** This is Discovery under Law VI, resolved in fiction and previously untested by anyone, because no one else has ever had access to one (Profile Section 12.5, Healing and Mana potions are System-exclusive). Owen's immediate question — *how many of those can you make?* — is unanswered.

**State at the close of this Event.** Alexander: Health 144/144, Mana 72/72 (recovered to cap in active mode across the span), XP 740/1000, gold 1,052, 0 E-Rank crystals, 12 E-Rank cores, no injury, no offensive action taken. Owen: uninjured, capacity restored to full, Stone Skin [D-Rank] Novice known and once used. **Remaining in the instance: the boss (160 Health) and a second elite (80 Health), both at the shrine, neither yet engaged.** Instance closes 15:55 or on the boss kill; Owen is due at Edgewater at 16:00."
```

    domain: gatefall.skill_formation
    result: none
description: "The promotion barrier for Checkpoint 0036, discharging Profile Section 7.1's deferred non-combat half for the play Events since the `EVT-000175` barrier (`EVT-000176` through `EVT-000180`).

**Classification: `none`.** `EVT-000176` is a relationship scene and a play-fight in open water — no bounded objective, no informative resistance, and the one resolved roll in it was Owen's, not Alexander's. `EVT-000177` is conversation and a meal. `EVT-000178` is instruction *delivered*, not practised, and carries no tested execution by Alexander. `EVT-000179`'s two reads are Keen Sense and Exploit Pattern, both known ratified skills settling as Section 7.4 use counters rather than formation candidates.

**`EVT-000180` is deliberately not classified here.** It is an open dangerous scene — the boss and a second elite are alive and the instance has not closed — and Section 7.1 settles danger once, at scene close. Its formation audit, its qualifying-scene credit for Keen Sense, Exploit Pattern and Field Command, and any candidate movement on `formation_instruction` (currently tracking at 1/3, and a plausible match for this afternoon's coached clear) all remain outstanding and are owed at that close. Recording this explicitly so the obligation survives the checkpoint.

**Combat-formation reconciliation for the span** (Section 7.4 promotion reconciliation, bounded to play since `EVT-000175`): re-counted from resolved actions and the Mana trace. Alexander's trace reads 72 to 70 to 72, one activation, matching one Keen Sense use exactly. Exploit Pattern: one activation, one use. Field Command: three resolved actions in which the relayed Pattern materially applied. **Overpower is explicitly counted as zero for `EVT-000176`** — a non-hostile partner in play, and a grapple that was broken rather than resolved, which fails Section 7.4's material-application test. No missed count, duplicate, or stale threshold found. No `pending-ratification` candidate exists; the readiness gate is clear.

No fictional time, roll, Stat, pool, item, or resolved outcome changed.

**Recovery derivation for the span, restored 2026-07-30 from checkpoint 0042's still-immutable copy after a later ledger compression dropped it from live state without a surviving live copy.** 3h37m elapsed from the 2026-08-06 11:15 anchor, all of it `active` mode. Health was at maximum throughout the whole span and never took damage — Alexander made no attack and was never attacked — so its remainder stays 0 (Profile Section 6.1.1). Mana: full at 11:15 and untouched until the instance threshold at ~13:52, where a single Keen Sense activation cost 2 (72 → 70, `EVT-000179`). From 13:52 to 14:52, 3,600s active: 0 + 3,600 × 72 × 20 = 5,184,000 units → +7 Mana, which caps the pool at 72 and resets the remainder to 0 per Section 5.2 (Mana 70 → 72) — one activation in the trace, matching exactly the one recorded Keen Sense use above. The instance's own clock is separate from recovery: it closes at 15:55 real elapsed time from its 13:52 opening, or on the boss kill."
```

## EVT-000182 - The Shrine Guard, and What Was Under It

```yaml
id: EVT-000182
canonical_record: REC-000079
schema_version: "0.1.5"
status: active
provenance:
  source: transcript
  game_date: "2026-08-06 ~14:52-15:02 -05:00"
  real_date: "2026-07-29"
type: Event
kind: combat
importance: major
game_date: "2026-08-06 ~14:52-15:02 -05:00"
participants:
  - ENT-000125
  - ENT-000139
counter_deltas:
  - { subject: ENT-000125, counter: skills.keen_sense.successful_uses, delta: 1 }
description: "Continuing inside the Overgrown Temple instance from Checkpoint 0036's anchor.

**The economics, stated plainly.** Owen asked how many Mana potions Alexander could produce. Alexander's answer — *for you, anything* — was followed by the arithmetic: 75 g per Standard potion against roughly 180 g of crystal from an E-Rank clear, so two per official Gate, and **net negative on a private instance**, where the key alone costs 500 g. Owen drew the whole chain unaided, and it is the most consequential thing he worked out all day. **Potions are bought with gold; gold comes only from selling crystal to the shop; crystal comes from clears; and a private instance runs net negative at 500 g a key against roughly 180 g of crystal. Therefore the only route that actually funds a potion supply is official, contracted Gate work.** The pocket gates have never been about gold at all — they are practice, paid for out of contract money.

**And he stated the contradiction that puts him in, out loud and unprompted:** *everything you just described funds itself off official Gates, which means the version of me that's actually useful is the version where you keep taking contracts — and I have spent two weeks quietly hoping you'd take fewer.* He explicitly declined to resolve it, saying only that he would rather be the one who said it than the one who didn't.

**The further step is one he has plainly reached and has not yet said aloud**, and the distinction matters for what he can be treated as having committed to: a mender who wants a supply for his patients cannot be a mender who stays home. The supply is downstream of Gate work, so wanting it means wanting Alexander in Gates — and, before long, means being *in* them himself, sponsoring, staffing, or crewing the contracts that pay for it. Nothing has been proposed, agreed, or asked for. It is a conclusion sitting in the room with both of them, and it bears directly on **OBJ-12** (a crew led by Owen), **OBJ-18**, and the two weeks he has spent hoping for exactly the opposite. He then identified the implication that actually matters to him — a refillable mender breaks the four-or-five-patient ceiling that the entire profession's triage, rationing and crew planning are built on — and immediately afterward was told the constraint: **he may not give one to anyone else, not even another mender.** He did not argue it, named it as the right call, said he hated it, and named the specific person it costs (Ines, a colleague at Edgewater he has watched budget herself down to two patients on a bad night). **Character first: the first time Alexander's secret has directly forced Owen to withhold care he could have given.**

**Keen Sense on the shrine.** 2 Mana (72 -> 70), d100 31, net +2, effective **71 — success**. Forty metres closer than the threshold read and with empty bays between. Confirmed two occupants, matching the Pattern derived at `EVT-000179` from lattice density alone. The decisive finding is that both are **anchored**: they work a radius of roughly twelve metres centred on the shrine and do not leave it, which is why neither pursued Owen down the colonnade. The boundary belongs to them and not to the party — **Alexander and Owen can cross it in either direction at will; the garrison cannot follow them out.** The smaller occupant walks a fixed arc reaching the radius edge roughly every nine seconds, at which point it stands about seven metres from the boss with its back turned.

**Pattern capacity.** Exploit Pattern at Practiced supports two concurrent Patterns and both slots were full. The lattice Pattern had done its work — the bays were empty and the garrison fights on the floor — so it was dropped and **the anchor became the second active Pattern**. Rank-Sight contributed nothing and was recorded as contributing nothing: it reads a Gate's true Rank, and this instance's Rank was bought over a shop counter an hour earlier. No use counted for it.

**The second elite.** Alexander relayed the anchor Pattern by call and briefed the execution: enter at the nine-second window and only then, take the smaller occupant off the back of its arc, and if anything goes wrong walk out across the line rather than fight out, because the line is a wall for them and a doorway for him. Owen repeated it back clean, unprompted — a habit acquired this afternoon.

Resolution: d100 63, net +2 (Rank gap, relayed Pattern, an unseen strike at a chosen window against a turned back), effective clamp(103) = **100 — legendary success.** He crossed at a walk rather than a run because a run announces itself, took it at the base of the skull, and it was dead before the second half of it landed. **Kill XP 20 to Alexander** under Section 3.8 setup credit (E-Rank elite 10 x 2, no underdog multiplier at or below D-Rank): **XP 740 -> 760/1000.**

**The shrine answered.** Every rope in the vault contracted at once on the elite's death — a single hard closing — and the sap-motion inside them accelerated visibly. It had not reacted to any of the fourteen deaths out in the bays. The boss came off the block for the first time in twelve minutes and set itself **between Owen and the shrine rather than between Owen and the exit**, which is the behaviour that made the rest of the afternoon."
```

## EVT-000183 - The Boss, the Footwork, and the Lid

```yaml
id: EVT-000183
canonical_record: REC-000079
schema_version: "0.1.5"
status: active
provenance:
  source: transcript
  game_date: "2026-08-06 ~15:02-15:09 -05:00"
  real_date: "2026-07-29"
type: Event
kind: combat
importance: major
game_date: "2026-08-06 ~15:02-15:09 -05:00"
participants:
  - ENT-000125
  - ENT-000139
counter_deltas:
  - { subject: ENT-000125, counter: skills.flash_step.successful_uses, delta: 1 }
  - { subject: ENT-000125, counter: skills.exploit_pattern.successful_uses, delta: 1 }
  - { subject: ENT-000125, counter: skills.field_command.successful_uses, delta: 3 }
description: "The E-Rank boss (160 Health, x4 Rank Health) fought entirely by Owen Callahan until its last minute.

**Exchange one.** Owen engaged with Stone Skin raised before contact — d100 54, net +1, effective 74, **success**; damage `(250/4 + weapon power 2) x 0.75 x 1.0` = **48**, boss 160 -> 112. The boss answered d100 62, net -1, effective 42, **partial success** — and the partial is the finding: it delivered a driving shoulder rather than a killing strike, put him on his back four metres nearer the line, and then **declined the follow-up it had earned**, stepping back between him and the block instead. **12 damage through Stone Skin and the Horizon kit** (Owen 250 -> 238). It was not hunting him. It was refusing him ground. Owen was up inside two seconds, hands closed, still narrating — **none of the four agreed tells fired, and Alexander did not move.**

**Exchange two.** d100 90, net +1, effective **100 — legendary success**: he arrived on the recovery beat rather than the set — the exact correction Alexander diagnosed in Priya Okafor at `EVT-000116` — and put the Quickknife back through the wound he had already opened. Damage `(62.5 + 2) x 0.75 x 2.0` = **97**, boss 112 -> **15**. The boss's answer d100 47, net -1, effective 27, **failure**: a wild sweep off a foreleg that no longer held, carrying itself around on its own miss.

**The lid.** The ropes then went slack — all of them at once, the sap draining back down into the stone — and the shrine came clear of the growth that had dressed it since entry. **It is a lid.** A seam runs the full width of the block. It had not been openable while the second elite lived.

**Alexander enters the fight he swore off, on his own initiative and for a different reason.** He confirmed to Owen that the room ends when the boss dies — Section 17, no post-boss window for an instant dungeon, the ruling this campaign already made at `EVT-000097` — so the lid, the eleven unmined deposit crystals, and the room itself were on one clock with no second chance and no second key to this instance.

**Flash Step displacement.** 5 Mana (70 -> 65 after settled recovery), d100 **99 — a natural critical success**, read from the natural roll and unmodifiable (Rules Section 4.6). He crossed twelve metres instantly onto the boss's blind quarter, angled so the only way it could face him was by turning off the stone. The boss's response d100 94, net -2, effective 54, **partial success** — it made the correct read and committed fully to the larger threat that had appeared a body-length away, **and that correct read cost it the thing it was guarding.** The seam stood bare eight metres from Owen with nothing between.

**The footwork.** Alexander then held it as the threat using the reactive-footwork method drilled cold with Milo (`ENT-000168`) at the Coalition training grounds two days earlier (`EVT-000167`) — d100 38, net +3 (overwhelming capability gap, Flash Step's Expert follow-up window, the drilled method, against the difficulty of holding rather than killing), effective **98 — exceptional success.** Shift, present, threaten, withdraw a half-beat before the commitment lands; arriving where the weight is going rather than where it is. **He never struck it once.** Its head did not turn for the noise behind it. The boss's answer d100 60, net -2, effective 20, **major failure**: it spent everything on one lunge, the ruined quarter folded mid-commitment, and the lunge became a collapse.

**The lid, opened.** Owen set his feet and slid it a foot and a half off a cavity cut square into the block. Ruling recorded at this Event: the Overgrown Temple archetype's authored payoff is the bonus loot roll on the clear, which settles regardless; **the lid is a separate feature the fiction produced and receives its own Section 11.2 boss-drop roll at a one-Rank bump**, justified under Section 11.4's principle that elevated danger is paid a Rank — the seam was sealed behind the extra elite the archetype adds and opened only by killing it. Rolled at D-Rank: **d100 72 — potion cache**; tier follows Rank (E-D -> lesser); mix d4 **1** -> **3 lesser healing potions.**

A garrison, an extra elite, and a sealed stone lid, for three lesser healing potions. Owen laughed until he had to sit back on his heels."
```

```yaml

  - { subject: ENT-000125, counter: skills.keen_sense.mastery_level, delta: 1 }
  - { subject: ENT-000125, counter: skills.exploit_pattern.qualifying_scenes_total, delta: 1 }
  - { subject: ENT-000125, counter: skills.exploit_pattern.mastery_progress, delta: -2 }
  - { subject: ENT-000125, counter: skills.exploit_pattern.mastery_level, delta: 1 }
